# PyMaCRUISE Porting Plan

## Progress and completion estimate

## Validation cadence

Batch adjacent source methods that share state and call order into one implementation slice. For each slice, run focused Java-reference tests and the closest integration tests; run the full repository suite at major workflow-integration milestones and final acceptance, or sooner when a change crosses algorithm families or shared image/geometry contracts. Record each validation command, pass/fail/skip counts, duration, and tested commit in the row or linked report. Count only newly mapped selected Java method lines toward source-line progress; when a cycle only connects already-counted operations, report a zero source-line delta alongside its integration progress.

Progress updates are for ongoing informal decision support, not a formal go/no-go. Show validated implementation changes, source-line delta, test evidence, remaining work, and blockers consistently over time so the project’s direction can be assessed from evidence. The first complete synthetic base workflow is a substantive progress update, not a pass/fail gate, deadline, or formal decision point. If validated progress is not visible over time, formal gates may be introduced later; none are established by this plan.

**Estimate date: 2026-10-05.** This is a planning estimate, not a validated performance or delivery commitment. The source-order-atlas base workflow completes and resumes synthetically; P41q now runs raw plus T1/FLAIR/mask inputs through a restartable terminal LesionTOADS checkpoint. Re-estimate after base convergence/source comparison and the downstream Dura-to-CRUISE handoff are integrated.

The brain-resolution six-stage synthetic base workflow completes and resumes with every stage verified/skipped. Its first TOADS pass reaches the source-configured 50-iteration cap without the `1e-4` energy threshold; the second converges at iteration 20. The first registered-loop transform is bit-identical to Java at shared inputs, but later growth/thinning and iteration-2 memberships still differ; whole-TOADS equivalence is not claimed. P41q now runs raw T1/FLAIR/user-mask inputs through preprocessing, atlas alignment/recrop, and restartable LesionTOADS terminal or continuing outputs. Its synthetic CLI test verifies stage order/hashes, channel order, RAS atlas-grid outputs, resume, corrupt-tail repair, and input/settings rejection. P41q is workflow composition from previously represented methods; it adds zero selected base-TOADS method lines. The updated full suite passed **1,193 tests, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 11 expected RAS warnings** on `7dc8a67` (995.58s, peak sampled RSS 14.609 GB under the 24 GB watchdog). Selected base-TOADS estimate remains 3,464–4,864 of 13,651 lines (about 25–36%); LesionTOADS lines are outside that denominator. Downstream Dura-to-CRUISE handoff, human-sized convergence, base/plus Java comparison, and real-MRI acceptance remain open. No human MRI was used. See [P41q report](docs/reports/plus-full-workflow-2026-10-05.md), [base-run report](docs/reports/base-workflow-brain-resolution-converged-2026-10-04.md), [terminal checkpoint report](docs/reports/plus-terminal-lesion-checkpoint-2026-10-05.md), and [full-suite report](docs/reports/full-pytest-2026-10-05-plus-full-workflow.md).

Status is summarized from the detailed acceptance rows below.

For detailed row-by-row evidence, including full-suite reports and tested commit hashes for Done rows, use the acceptance table and linked reports below. These statuses are intentionally narrower than “equivalent to the full MATLAB/JIST pipeline.”

This is the step-by-step implementation and acceptance plan for a Python-native MaCRUISE. Work proceeds from source contracts and synthetic cases to stage-by-stage ports, and only then to a small, approved set of real reference cases. Synthetic image volumes, phantoms, and expected outputs created for this project are intended to be committed under `tests/data/synthetic/`; no human imaging data belongs in that directory.

## Completion definition

The port is complete when the base MaCRUISE workflow (T1 + MaSeg) and the selected MaCRUISEplus workflow (T1 + MaSeg/TOADS inputs + FLAIR, as defined by the source entry point) can be installed, configured, run, resumed, and produce documented volume and surface outputs without MATLAB or a MIPAV/JIST JVM runtime. Each stage must have automated checks. Synthetic cases must exercise the contracts and edge cases before reference MRI is introduced. Final equivalence must be assessed against reproducible MATLAB/JIST outputs from approved reference cases, with per-output tolerances, metrics, and any known deviations recorded in the repository.

“Reasonable numerical error” is not one tolerance for every output: exact label/mask operations should be exact; floating-point image stages require measured, operation-specific tolerances; surfaces and thickness require geometric/statistical tolerances. Proposed criteria in the table are minimum gates. Establish and record the final per-stage tolerances against the reference implementation before claiming equivalence.

## Working rules

- Execute rows in dependency order. A row is **validated** only when its stated checks pass and evidence (test name/report, metrics, or source decision) is recorded. Do not fill a validation date before that evidence exists.
- For every **Done** row, record validation appropriate to its scope: focused tests and the closest affected integration tests for localized changes; a full-repository `pytest` run only at major workflow-integration milestones and final acceptance, or sooner when a change crosses multiple algorithm families or shared image/geometry contracts. Include the exact command, pass/fail/skip counts, duration, and tested commit in the row or its linked report. Existing full-suite reports remain valid historical evidence for their stated commits; do not rerun the multi-minute suite merely to close a localized row.
- Keep test data synthetic until the Synthetic Readiness Gate. That gate requires fixtures for geometry, label maps, tissue boundaries, bias fields, transforms, surfaces, and lesion cases, and automated tests that pass without downloading or mounting subject data.
- Commit deterministic synthetic input volumes and small expected-result fixtures. Generate large or derivable fixtures in tests when doing so is simpler and deterministic; commit the generator and its seed. Every fixture must have a description, dimensions, spacing/affine, value or label meaning, provenance (synthetic), and license note where relevant.
- Use the MATLAB/JIST wrappers as workflow/parameter contracts and VandyCRUISE as the selected algorithm reference. Reuse NiBabel, NumPy, SciPy, ANTsPy, and scikit-image for general operations where behavior matches. Select mesh I/O/geometry tools only as needed for scientific surface outputs; do not add rendering/viewer tools to the port. Do not substitute a generic algorithm for TOADS, topology correction, ACE, GVF, or CRUISE/TGDM without equivalence evidence.
- Do not require FreeSurfer to install, configure, run, validate, or use PyMaCRUISE. Do not require FreeSurfer data, licenses, or outputs; historical references in source notes are explanatory only.
- Store every image file managed by the project as compressed NIfTI (`.nii.gz`): bundled image assets, synthetic fixtures, persisted inputs, intermediates, and outputs. Normalize images supplied in other formats before storing or reusing them. Keep text/configuration/mesh formats appropriate to their non-image role.
- Express all spatial outputs in RAS: volume voxel axes use RAS orientation and surface/point coordinates use RAS+ millimetres. Reorient every non-RAS spatial input before use, display a warning, and record the input frame/orientation and conversion in the run manifest.
- Run real-data checks only after synthetic unit/stage gates pass. Use a small, de-identified, approved reference set; do not commit those images or outputs. Record access-controlled locations and hashes/identifiers only where policy permits.

## Step-by-step acceptance table

Validation evidence for completed rows is recorded in the row or a linked report under `docs/reports/`. Each entry names the row-specific focused and/or integration checks and the tested commit; major integration gates and final acceptance also cite the corresponding full-suite run. Historical rows may retain full-suite evidence from their original implementation commits.

Dates use `YYYY-MM-DD`. `—` means not started/not yet validated. Status values: **Done** (documentation/planning artifact complete), **Started**, **Blocked**, and **Not started**. “Done” on the plan row means the plan exists; it does not imply implementation or validation.

## Real-data gate and data handling

Do not use actual imaging data to fill gaps in ordinary development when the same contract can be tested synthetically. P35 is the required readiness review. Actual MRI is reserved for checking biological realism, whole-workflow interactions, and equivalence to the historical pipeline; it cannot replace deterministic synthetic unit/stage tests. Real cases and derived images stay outside Git unless the user explicitly approves an appropriate, shareable dataset and all applicable data-use requirements are satisfied.

## Progress recording

When work begins on a row, set status to **Started** and enter its start date. When its validation criteria pass, record the evidence briefly in the relevant source/test/report, set status to **Done**, and enter the validation date. If work is blocked by missing source/assets/behavior, name the blocker in the row or the decision log rather than marking the step complete. Keep `readme.md` as a short entry point and current summary; this file is the authoritative detailed roadmap.
